# Annex B — Index by Class and Sub-class for 4,818 Verbs

_Extracted from `docs/spanish_verbs_made_simpler.pdf`, pages 228–285 (printed pages 215–272), Annex B._

Each Spanish verb is mapped to the **conjugation model** it follows. The **Model (sub-class)** column names the parent model and, in parentheses, the specific sub-class whose irregularity it inherits (e.g. `cantar (tocar)` = the regular `-ar` model `cantar`, tocar sub-class for the c→qu spelling change). The **#** column is the book's model number for that (sub-)class. This is the mapping needed to assign a model to each verb when porting the parsimonious model scheme to Conjugar.

Conventions (from the annex preamble):

- Alphabetized per post-1994 rules: `ch` and `ll` are treated as the letter pairs `c+h`, `l+l`; `ñ` remains a separate letter following `n`.
- **DEF** (defective): the verb is normally used only in certain conjugations (infinitive, participles, and third person).
- A verb shown with `(se)` (e.g. `arrepentir(se)`) is used only reflexively.
- Homonyms with different conjugations are disambiguated as `(1)` / `(2)` (see footnotes).
- Superscript footnote markers in the source are rendered here in the **Note** column; see [Footnotes](#footnotes).

**Total verbs:** 4818

## Footnotes

<a id="fn1"></a>**1.** Frequently conjugated without written accents (e.g., *agrio* rather than *agrió*).

<a id="fn2"></a>**2.** To bet.

<a id="fn3"></a>**3.** To station or post.

<a id="fn4"></a>**4.** To raze, devastate.

<a id="fn5"></a>**5.** To burn up, parch.

<a id="fn6"></a>**6.** To demolish.

<a id="fn7"></a>**7.** To terrify.

<a id="fn8"></a>**8.** To stuff.

<a id="fn9"></a>**9.** To attest.

<a id="fn10"></a>**10.** The new orthographic rules allow alternative forms for the simple past 1s and 3s—*crie/crié, crio/crió*—as well as for the present indicative and subjunctive 2p—*criais/criáis* and *crieis/criéis*.

<a id="fn11"></a>**11.** Past participle: *desprovisto/desproveído*.

<a id="fn12"></a>**12.** Can also be conjugated without diphthongs, like *cantar*.

<a id="fn13"></a>**13.** The new orthographic rules allow alternative forms for the simple past 1s and 3s—*fie/fié, fio/fió*—as well as for the present indicative and subjunctive 2p—*fiais/fiáis* and *fieis/fiéis*.

<a id="fn14"></a>**14.** The new orthographic rules allow alternative forms for the simple past 1s—*flui/fluí*—as well as for the present indicative 2p—*fluis/fluís*.

<a id="fn15"></a>**15.** Past participle: *frito/freído*.

<a id="fn16"></a>**16.** The new orthographic rules allow alternative forms for the simple past 1s and 3s—*guie/guié, guio/guió*—as well as for the present indicative and subjunctive 2p—*guiais/guiáis* and *guieis/guiéis*.

<a id="fn17"></a>**17.** The new orthographic rules allow alternative forms for the simple past 1s—*hui/huí*—as well as for the present indicative 2p—*huis/huís*.

<a id="fn18"></a>**18.** The new orthographic rules allow alternative forms for the simple past 1s and 3s—*lie/lié, lio/lió*—as well as for the present indicative and subjunctive 2p—*liais/liáis* and *lieis/liéis*.

<a id="fn19"></a>**19.** The new orthographic rules allow alternative forms for the simple past 1s and 3s—*pie/pié, pio/pió*—as well as for the present indicative and subjunctive 2p—*piais/piáis* and *pieis/piéis*.

<a id="fn20"></a>**20.** Conjugation identical to that of *pudrir* apart from infinitive.

<a id="fn21"></a>**21.** Past participle: *provisto/proveído*.

<a id="fn22"></a>**22.** Past participle: *refrito/refreído*.

<a id="fn23"></a>**23.** In conjugations where stem syllable is stressed, ehu → ehú (as for 1-8 *rehusar*).

<a id="fn24"></a>**24.** Past participle: *sofrito/sofreído*.

<a id="fn25"></a>**25.** Exists in all six persons but not used in future, conditional, or imperative.
